Appendix A
Self-consistency of HEFT

In this appendix, we focus on terms containing two-matter-particle cuts on which the HEFT integrand factorizes into lower point HEFT integrands. We argue such terms require quantum information about the HEFT tree amplitudes used in the unitarity construction.

We will use the
\hbar
counting as introduced in
eq.

2
. Consider a cut of a four-scalar amplitude containing
k
HEFT tree amplitudes, each with
\tilde{n}_{g,i}
internal gravitons
n_{g,i}
external gravitons, and
n_{\phi,i}
scalars; call
\tilde{N}_{g}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\tilde{n}_{g,i}
and
n_{g}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}n_{g,i}
respectively the total number of internal and external gravitons, and
N_{\phi}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}n_{\phi,i}-2
the total number of internal scalars. Restoring the integration measure and the matter and graviton propagators, this cut scales as

\displaystyle\underbrace{\Bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{k}\hbar^{-3(\frac{1}{2}n_{\phi,i}-1)-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{n}_{g,i}-\frac{1}{2}n_{g,i}}\Bigg]}_{\text{HEFT tree amplitudes}}\underbrace{\vphantom{\Bigg[}\hbar^{-2\tilde{N}_{g}-N_{\phi}}\hbar^{4L}}_{\text{propagators and measures}}=\hbar^{-3-\frac{1}{2}n_{g}}\hbar^{\tilde{N}_{g}+1-k}\,,

(213)

where we have used
eq.

3
and the loop number
L=\tilde{N}_{g}+N_{\phi}-k+1
. This cut scales classically if

\tilde{N}_{g}+1-k=0\ .

(214)

Consistency requires that if we cut any line the result should still scale classically. Let us consider cutting a pair of matter lines. The resulting eight-scalar amplitude scales as

\hbar^{-9-\frac{1}{2}n_{g}}\hbar^{\tilde{N}_{g}+1-k}\hbar^{8-a}\,.

(215)

Here we have isolated the classical scaling
\hbar^{-9}
of an eight-scalar amplitude.
If the graph remains connected, then we remove two matter propagators and two integral measures from
eq.

213
, which leads to
a=8
. On the other hand, if the graph is now disconnected, then we only need to remove two matter propagators and one integral measure instead, which leads to
a=4
.
Thus, if
a=8
– i.e. if the cut of the eight-scalar amplitude is connected – then it has classical scaling upon use of
eq.

214
and the truncation to classical order is iteratively consistent.
In contrast, if
a=4
– i.e. if the cut of the eight-scalar amplitude is disconnected – the cut is suppressed by
\hbar^{4}
, which means that the disconnected components contain quantum contributions.

Repeating the calculation above for any pair of matter lines, it follows that any 2MPR contribution constructible by generalized unitarity with a classical scaling requires quantum information about some amplitude contributing to it. Since the HEFT approach prescribes that tree amplitudes be truncated to classical orders, the
\hbar
scaling of 2MPR diagrams and cuts with classical vertices is super-classical, with one factor of
\hbar^{-1}
for each present two-matter-particle cut. On the other hand, if we explicitly include some quantum operators in the Lagrangian and keep higher-order terms in the HEFT tree amplitudes, we will get classical 2MPR contributions. Again, the above argument only applies to two-matter-particle cuts on which the HEFT integrand factorizes into lower point integrands.

Appendix B
Evaluating box master integrals

In this appendix, we derive all the box master integrals listed in
section

6.2
. We will repeatedly use the bubble integral with a cut matter propagator and a generic propagator in
d=4-2\epsilon
,

\displaystyle\int\textrm{d}^{4-2\epsilon}\ell\frac{\delta(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell-Q_{1})^{2}-m^{2}+i0}=-\frac{\pi^{(3-2\epsilon)/2}\Gamma(\frac{2\epsilon-1}{2})}{[-(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot Q_{1})^{2}+m^{2}-i0]^{(2\epsilon-1)/2}}\,,

(216)

where
Q_{1}
is a generic vector.

B.1
I_{1,1,1,0}

We first compute the box integral with three graviton propagators,

\displaystyle I_{1,1,1,0}=\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}(\ell+q_{2})^{2}[(\ell-q_{1})^{2}+i0]}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\textrm{d}x\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}{\ell}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}[(\ell-q_{1}+xk_{5})^{2}+i0]^{2}}\,,

(217)

where we have introduced a Feynman parameter to combine the two massless propagators
(\ell+q_{2})^{2}
and
(\ell-q_{1})^{2}
into a single propagator. We next use an IBP identity to reduce the double propagator
[(\ell-q_{1}+xk_{5})^{2}+i0]^{2}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\textrm{d}x\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}[(\ell-q_{1}+xk_{5})^{2}+i0]^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{d-3}{2(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\textrm{d}x\frac{1}{(1-x)^{2}[(1-x)q_{1}^{2}+xq_{2}^{2}]}\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell-q_{1}+xk_{5})^{2}+i0}

\displaystyle\quad-(d-4)\int_{0}^{1}\textrm{d}x\frac{1}{(1-x)q_{1}^{2}+xq_{2}^{2}}\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}[(\ell-q_{1}+xk_{5})^{2}+i0]}\,.

(218)

The integral in the last line is finite in
d=4
,
23
23

23

One can show this by using

\displaystyle\int\textrm{d}^{4}\ell\frac{\delta(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}[(\ell-Q_{1})^{2}-m^{2}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi^{2}}{\sqrt{(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot Q_{1})^{2}-m^{2}-Q_{1}^{2}}}\arcsin\sqrt{1+\frac{(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot Q_{1})^{2}-m^{2}}{-Q_{1}^{2}}}

for the
\ell
integral. The remaining
x
integral is convergent.

such that the
(d-4)
prefactor makes this term only contribute to
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
order. Thus both the IR divergence and the finite part are given by the first term of
section

B.1
,

\displaystyle I_{1,1,1,0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{d-3}{2(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\textrm{d}x\frac{1}{(1-x)^{2}[(1-x)q_{1}^{2}+xq_{2}^{2}]}\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell-q_{1}+xk_{5})^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{5/2-\epsilon}\Gamma(\epsilon+1/2)}{(2\pi)^{4-2\epsilon}[-(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}-i0]^{1/2+\epsilon}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\textrm{d}x}{(1-x)^{1+2\epsilon}[(1-x)q_{1}^{2}+xq_{2}^{2}]}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)

(219)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{32\pi q_{2}^{2}\sqrt{-(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}-i0}}\left[-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\text{IR}}}+\log(-4\pi(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}-i0)+2\log\frac{q_{2}^{2}}{q_{1}^{2}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\,.

The
i0
prescription suggests the analytic continuation
\log(-x-i0)=\log(x)-i\pi
for
x>0
and
\sqrt{-(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}-i0}=-i(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})
, which gives

\displaystyle I_{1,1,1,0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{32q_{2}^{2}(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})}+\frac{i}{32\pi q_{2}^{2}(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})}\left[-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\text{IR}}}+\log(4\pi(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2})+2\log\frac{q_{2}^{2}}{q_{1}^{2}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\,.

(220)

The result agrees with
eq.

142
in the main text.

B.2
I^{\pm}_{1,1,0,1}

We next consider the box integrals with one uncut matter propagator. We start with

\displaystyle I^{+}_{1,1,0,1}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}(\ell+q_{2})^{2}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell+i0)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\textrm{d}x\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell+q_{2})^{2}[(\ell+x\bar{u}_{1})^{2}-x^{2}+i0]^{2}}\,,

(221)

where we have used the
eq.

134
to combine
\ell^{2}
and
2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell+i0
.
The IBP identity can reduce the double propagator

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\textrm{d}x\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell+q_{2})^{2}[(\ell+x\bar{u}_{1})^{2}-x^{2}+i0]^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{d-3}{2(y^{2}-1)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\textrm{d}x\frac{1}{x^{2}(2x\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2}-q_{2}^{2}-i0)}\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell+x\bar{u}_{1})^{2}-x^{2}+i0}

\displaystyle\quad+(d-4)\int_{0}^{\infty}\textrm{d}x\frac{1}{2x\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2}-q_{2}^{2}}\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}[(\ell-q_{2}+x\bar{u}_{1})^{2}-x^{2}+i0]}\,.

(222)

The integral proportional to
d-4
contributes to
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
order, for a reason similar to the discussion below
section

B.1
. Now using
eq.

216
for the bubble integral in the second line of
section

B.2
, we get the IR divergence and the finite part of this integral,

\displaystyle I^{+}_{1,1,0,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{d-3}{2(y^{2}-1)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\textrm{d}x\frac{1}{x^{2}(2x\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2}-q_{2}^{2}-i0)}\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell+x\bar{u}_{1})^{2}-x^{2}+i0}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi^{5/2-\epsilon}\Gamma(\epsilon+1/2)}{(2\pi)^{4-2\epsilon}[-(y^{2}-1)-i0]^{1/2+\epsilon}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\textrm{d}x}{x^{1+2\epsilon}(2x\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2}-q_{2}^{2}-i0)}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{32\pi q_{2}^{2}\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\left[-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\text{IR}}}+\log(-\pi(y^{2}-1)-i0)-2\log\frac{\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2}-i0}{-q_{2}^{2}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{32\pi q_{2}^{2}\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\left[-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\text{IR}}}+\log(-\pi(y^{2}-1)-i0)-2\log\frac{\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2}}{-q_{2}^{2}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\,.

(223)

Here we can omit the
i0
prescription in
\log\frac{\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2}}{-q_{2}^{2}}
because the argument is positive. To compute
I^{-}_{1,1,0,1}
, we first redefine the loop momentum to flip the sign of
i0
,

\displaystyle I^{-}_{1,1,0,1}=\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}(\ell+q_{2})^{2}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell-i0)}=-\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}(\ell-q_{2})^{2}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell+i0)}\,.

(224)

We can directly obtain the result of this integral by sending
q_{2}\rightarrow-q_{2}
in
section

B.2
while preserving the sign of
i0
,

\displaystyle I^{-}_{1,1,0,1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i}{32\pi q_{2}^{2}\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\left[-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\text{IR}}}+\log(-\pi(y^{2}-1)-i0)-2\log\frac{-\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2}-i0}{-q_{2}^{2}}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16q_{2}^{2}\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}-I^{+}_{1,1,0,1}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\,.

(225)

Therefore, the combination
I^{+}_{1,1,0,1}+I^{-}_{1,1,0,1}
is free of IR divergences,

\displaystyle I^{+}_{1,1,0,1}+I^{-}_{1,1,0,1}=\frac{1}{16q_{2}^{2}\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\,,

(226)

which reproduces
eq.

143a
.

B.3
I^{\pm}_{1,0,1,1}

We apply the same strategy to compute the last set of box integrals,

\displaystyle I^{\pm}_{1,0,1,1}=\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}(\ell-q_{1})^{2}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell\pm i0)}\,.

(227)

The only difference here is that the combination
I^{+}_{1,0,1,1}+I^{-}_{1,0,1,1}
is now complex, with the imaginary part given by further cutting
(\ell-q_{1})^{2}
. Starting with
I^{+}_{1,0,1,1}
, we first apply
eq.

134
to combine
(\ell-q_{1})^{2}
and
2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell+i0
, followed by using an IBP identity to get,

\displaystyle I^{+}_{1,0,1,1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\textrm{d}x\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}[(\ell-q_{1}+x\bar{u}_{1})^{2}-x^{2}+i0]^{2}}\equiv I_{a}^{+}+I_{b}^{+}\,,

(228)

where
I_{a}^{+}
and
I_{b}^{-}
are defined as

\displaystyle I_{a}^{+}

\displaystyle=-\frac{d-3}{2q_{1}^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\textrm{d}x\frac{1}{(y^{2}-1)x^{2}-2(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})yx+(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell-q_{1}+x\bar{u}_{1})^{2}-x^{2}+i0}\,,

\displaystyle I_{b}^{+}

\displaystyle=-\frac{d-4}{q_{1}^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\textrm{d}x\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}[(\ell-q_{1}+x\bar{u}_{1})^{2}-x^{2}+i0]}\,.

(229)

Similar to
eq.

224
, we can rewrite
I^{-}_{1,0,1,1}
as

\displaystyle I^{-}_{1,0,1,1}=\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}(\ell-q_{1})^{2}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell-i0)}

\displaystyle=-\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}(\ell+q_{1})^{2}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell+i0)}

\displaystyle\equiv-(I^{-}_{a}+I^{-}_{b})\,,

(230)

where
I^{-}_{a,b}=I^{+}_{a,b}\big|_{q_{1}\rightarrow-q_{1}}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle I^{+}_{1,0,1,1}+I^{-}_{1,0,1,1}=(I^{+}_{a}-I^{-}_{a})+(I^{+}_{b}-I^{-}_{b})\,,

(231)

such that we only need to compute the combination
I^{+}_{a}-I^{-}_{a}
and
I^{+}_{b}-I^{-}_{b}
.

If we use
eq.

216
with
d=4
to integrate out the bubble in
I_{a}^{\pm}
, we will find that the resulting
x
integral is divergent at
x\rightarrow\infty
. However, by taking the difference
I^{+}_{a}-I^{-}_{a}
, we get a finite result since the range of
x
gets truncated. Interestingly, the result is proportional to the triangle integral (
139
),

\displaystyle I_{a}^{+}-I_{a}^{-}=\frac{1}{q_{1}^{2}}(I^{+}_{0,0,1,1}+I^{-}_{0,0,1,1})=\frac{1}{16q_{1}^{2}\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}+\frac{i}{8\pi q_{1}^{2}\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\arccosh(y)\,.

(232)

To compute
I^{+}_{b}-I^{-}_{b}
, we introduce another Feynman parameter
w
and perform the
\ell
integral in
d=4
. The result is finite due to the same truncation on the range of
x
,

\displaystyle I^{+}_{b}-I^{-}_{b}=-\frac{d-4}{16\pi^{2}q_{1}^{2}}\int_{-\alpha}^{\alpha}\textrm{d}x\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\textrm{d}w}{[-w^{2}(x^{2}+\beta)(y^{2}-1)-w(1-w)q_{1}^{2}-i0]^{1/2}}\,,

(233)

such that
I^{+}_{b}-I^{-}_{b}=0
in
d=4
. Therefore, we have reproduced
eq.

143b
,

\displaystyle I^{+}_{1,0,1,1}+I^{-}_{1,0,1,1}=I^{+}_{a}-I^{-}_{a}=\frac{1}{16q_{1}^{2}\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}+\frac{i}{8\pi q_{1}^{2}\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\arccosh(y)\,.

(234)

B.4
I^{\pm}_{0,1,1,1}

We first use the same Feynman parameterization as in
eq.

217
to bring together the two massless propagators,

\displaystyle I^{+}_{0,1,1,1}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell+q_{2})^{2}(\ell-q_{1})^{2}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell+i0)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\textrm{d}x\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell-q_{1}+xk_{5})^{4}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell+i0)}\,.

(235)

The resultant double propagator
(\ell-q_{1}+xk_{5})^{4}
is then reduced by the IBP relation,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\textrm{d}x\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell-q_{1}+xk_{5})^{4}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell+i0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{d-3}{4(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\textrm{d}x\frac{x(\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2})+(1-x)y(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})}{(1-x)^{2}\mathcal{F}(x)}\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell-q_{1}+xk_{5})^{2}+i0}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{d-4}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\textrm{d}x\frac{y^{2}-1}{\mathcal{G}(x)}\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell-q_{1}+xk_{5})^{2}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell+i0)}\,,

(236)

where
\mathcal{G}(x)
is a quadratic polynomial in
x
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(x)=x^{2}(\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2})^{2}+2x(1-x)y(\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2})(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})+(1-x)^{2}(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}\,.

(237)

Under the principal value combination
I^{+}_{0,1,1,1}+I^{-}_{0,1,1,1}
, the first integral in
section

B.4
is doubled while the second integral is finite in
d=4
, which only contributes to
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
due to the prefactor. We can now reproduce
eq.

144
through a direct integration,

\displaystyle I^{+}_{0,1,1,1}+I^{-}_{0,1,1,1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi^{5/2-\epsilon}\Gamma(\epsilon+1/2)}{(2\pi)^{4-2\epsilon}[-(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}-i0]^{1/2+\epsilon}}\int_{0}^{1}\textrm{d}x\frac{x(\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2})+(1-x)y(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})}{(1+x)^{1+2\epsilon}\mathcal{F}(x)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{32(\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2})(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})}

(238)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{i}{32\pi(\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2})(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})}\left[-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\text{IR}}}+\log(4\pi(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2})+2\log\frac{\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2}}{\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1}}\right]\,.

Appendix C
Master integrals determining the cut contribution

In this appendix, we list all the double-cut master integrals,

\displaystyle I^{\text{cut,cut}}_{a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}}=\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{i\,\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell)\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{[\ell^{2}]^{a_{1}}[(\ell+q_{2})^{2}]^{a_{2}}[(\ell-q_{1})^{2}]^{a_{3}}}\ ,

(239)

necessary for Sec.
9.1
. Due to the two delta functions, the dimension of the integral is reduced by two and the integrals are easy to evaluate directly using the same computation strategies as appendix
B
. Therefore, we simply summarize the results below:

\displaystyle\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell)\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell-q_{1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon)}{(4\pi)^{1-\epsilon}}\left(\frac{y^{2}-1}{(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}}\right)^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{16\pi\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\gamma+\log(4\pi)-\log\frac{(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}}{y^{2}-1}\right)

(240)

\displaystyle\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell)\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell+q_{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon)}{(4\pi)^{1-\epsilon}}\left(\frac{y^{2}-1}{(\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2})^{2}}\right)^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{16\pi\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\gamma+\log(4\pi)-\log\frac{(\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2})^{2}}{y^{2}-1}\right)

(241)

\displaystyle\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell)\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{(\ell-q_{1})^{2}(\ell+q_{2})^{2}}=\frac{\arccosh(y)}{16\pi(\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2})(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})}

(242)

\displaystyle\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell)\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}(\ell-q_{1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon)}{(4\pi)^{1-\epsilon}(-q_{1}^{2})^{1+\epsilon}}{}_{2}F_{1}\Big[\begin{array}[]{cc}-\epsilon,1+\epsilon\\
1-\epsilon\end{array},1+\frac{(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}}{q_{1}^{2}(y^{2}-1)}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16\pi q_{1}^{2}\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\left[\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\gamma+\log(4\pi)-\log\frac{q_{1}^{4}(y^{2}-1)}{(\bar{u}_{2}\cdot q_{1})^{2}}\right]

(245)

\displaystyle\int\frac{\textrm{d}^{d}\ell}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{1}\cdot\ell)\hat{\delta}(2\bar{u}_{2}\cdot\ell)}{\ell^{2}(\ell+q_{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\frac{\Gamma(\epsilon)}{(4\pi)^{1-\epsilon}(-q_{2}^{2})^{1+\epsilon}}{}_{2}F_{1}\Big[\begin{array}[]{cc}-\epsilon,1+\epsilon\\
1-\epsilon\end{array},1+\frac{(\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2})^{2}}{q_{2}^{2}(y^{2}-1)}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16\pi q_{2}^{2}\sqrt{y^{2}-1}}\left[\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\gamma+\log(4\pi)-\log\frac{q_{2}^{4}(y^{2}-1)}{(\bar{u}_{1}\cdot q_{2})^{2}}\right]

(248)

Some of the integrals listed here have also been evaluated in Ref.
Caron-Huot:2023vxl
.
